For state we want to preserve: either thread it through `_folderState` + persist on change + restore at the end of `ReloadConnections` (the pattern used by `ExpandedNodeIds` and `LastActiveConnectionId`), or hold it on the owner (`MainWindowViewModel`) where it doesn't share the tree's lifetime. **Rule**: when adding any new VM-side state on a tree node, decide explicitly which side of the rebuild boundary it lives on — defaults-restored is fine for transient flags (`IsDragging`, `IsDropTarget`), but persistence is mandatory for anything the user spent effort on.

38. **`x:DataType` on a `<Style>` does NOT scope the selector at runtime — it's a compile-time binding-typing hint only.** A `<Style Selector="TreeViewItem" x:DataType="vm:Foo">` matches *every* `TreeViewItem`, not just those whose `DataContext` is `Foo`. Multiple such styles for different VM types therefore all apply to every container, and the last one in document order wins; its compiled binding fails the `DataContext` cast for the non-matching types and clobbers the property with `UnsetValue`. **Rule**: for a container style (e.g. `TreeViewItem`) that must bind a property shared across several `DataContext` types, use ONE style with a `ReflectionBinding` (resolves by name, no cast), not N compiled-binding styles. This *supersedes the original gotcha #3*, whose "use N concrete-typed styles, one per VM type" advice was the actual cause of the auto-expand failure.

39. **When a UI binding "doesn't work", prove which of the three layers is broken before touching timing.** The three layers: (1) VM property actually changing, (2) the target control's binding receiving the change, (3) the control/container existing at all. A headless Avalonia probe (`HeadlessUnitTestSession.StartNew` + build the real window + `Dispatcher.UIThread.RunJobs()` + inspect the live control) isolates which layer fails in seconds, without a live DB or manual clicking. Reach for it instead of stacking `Dispatcher.Post` / property-toggle workarounds — those only ever paper over layer-1/2 confusion and leave the real defect in place.

40. **Pointer-event drag & drop, not the `DragDrop` API, for `TreeView` in Avalonia 12.** The built-in `DragDrop`/`DataObject` API is unreliable on a virtualized `TreeView`: item containers come and go under `VirtualizingStackPanel`, `DragOver`/`Drop` fire while the cursor is over child rows rather than the intended row, and there's no clean drop-position (before/after/into) signal. The working approach (sidebar tree): tunneled `PointerPressed` to record the drag candidate before `TreeView`'s own selection runs, an 8-px movement threshold before entering drag mode, `TreeView.InputHitTest(point)` + `FindAncestorOfType<TreeViewItem>(includeSelf:true)` to resolve the row VM under the pointer each `PointerMoved`, top/bottom-half of the target's translated `Bounds` for Before/After (folders → Into), and `PointerReleased`/`PointerCaptureLost` to commit or cancel. Visual feedback rides VM flags (`IsDragging`/`IsDropTarget`) bound to a Style class, not adorners. **Rule**: reach for pointer events whenever a drag needs per-row position resolution inside a virtualized items control.

41. **One-way converters: return `BindingOperations.DoNothing` from `ConvertBack`, never `throw new NotSupportedException()`.** WPF's idiom is to throw; Avalonia's pipeline catches it cleanly but Visual Studio's "Break when this exception type is not handled by user code" — on by default for `NotSupportedException` — surfaces it as a debugger prompt. Worse, `DataGridTextColumn` wires its `Binding` as TwoWay even when the grid is `IsReadOnly="True"` (the same `IBinding` instance drives both the display `TextBlock` and the editor `TextBox`); refresh / column reorder / sort paths invoke `ConvertBack` defensively. `BindingOperations.DoNothing` is the explicit "no write" signal the binding layer expects — no exception, no break.

42. **DataGrid sorting with compiled bindings requires explicit `SortMemberPath` on EVERY column.** With `x:DataType` on the host (compiled bindings), `DataGridTextColumn.Binding` is compiled to a delegate and its path string is opaque to the DataGrid's sort engine. `CanUserSortColumns="True"` enables header-click sorting, but without `SortMemberPath` the column silently no-ops on click — no arrow, no reorder. Add `SortMemberPath="<PropertyName>"` to every column, duplicating the `Binding` path on `DataGridTextColumn`s and providing it standalone on `DataGridTemplateColumn`s. **Rule**: when the host UserControl has `x:DataType`, treat `SortMemberPath` as mandatory boilerplate on every sortable column, not an optional override.

43. **Avalonia 12.0.3 `DataGrid` has no double-click-on-gripper auto-fit, and `DataGridColumnHeader.OwningColumn` is `internal`.** WPF/IBExpert/Excel users expect "double-click the column separator to auto-fit"; Avalonia 12 doesn't ship it. Implementing it from scratch is harder than expected: catching the bubbled `DoubleTappedEvent` and walking to an ancestor `Thumb` named `PART_RightHeaderGripper` is doable, but there's no public API to map the header back to its column (`OwningColumn` is internal — reflection works to read it, but setting `column.Width = DataGridLength.Auto` afterward didn't actually re-fit in our tests; the column either kept its width or measured to the same value). Defer until a future Avalonia release exposes either column-header `DoubleTapped` or a public `OwningColumn` + reliable Auto re-measure path.

51. **`Avalonia 12.0.3 CalendarDatePicker.SelectedDate` is `DateTime?`, NOT `DateTimeOffset?`.** Specs / older Avalonia samples assume `DateTimeOffset?`. In 12.0.3 the property is `DateTime?`; assigning a `DateTimeOffset?` is a `CS0029` compile error. The `DateTimeToDateTimeOffsetConverter` we ship is forward-compat for newer Avalonia versions or third-party pickers that do use `DateTimeOffset`; the view code path assigns `DateTime?` directly. **Rule**: check the API surface against the running Avalonia version before threading a converter for type bridging — the type may already match.

52. **`TextBox` scroll-bar visibility goes through attached `ScrollViewer.*` properties, not direct ones.** `<TextBox HorizontalScrollBarVisibility="Auto" .../>` fails with `AVLN2000` in 12.0.3 — the property doesn't exist on TextBox itself. Correct: `<TextBox ScrollViewer.HorizontalScrollBarVisibility="Auto" ScrollViewer.VerticalScrollBarVisibility="Auto" .../>` — these are the standard Avalonia ScrollViewer attached properties. **Rule**: when a scroll affordance is needed on a TextBox, reach for the attached `ScrollViewer.*` setters; don't expect them as direct properties.

58. **`DataGridLength.Auto` w Avalonia 12.0.3 nie zawsze respektuje header text width.** Dokumentacja mówi "max(SizeToCells, SizeToHeader)", ale dla kolumn z pustą lub krótką zawartością pierwszy measure-pass zwija column do minimum header'a — bez uwzględnienia jego pełnej szerokości tekstowej. Workaround: ustawić `MinWidth` per-kolumna obliczone jako ~7px × długość header'a + padding. **Reguła w projekcie**: każda kolumna DataGridowa otrzymuje explicit `MinWidth` chroniący header.

59. **FluentTheme DataGridCell ma DWIE warstwy podświetlenia, obie domyślnie biorą SystemAccent.** Pierwsza — focus rectangle (`Rectangle x:Name="FocusVisual"` w template, koloruje się przez `DataGridCellFocusVisualPrimaryBrush`/`Secondary`). Druga — cell-level background fill (`DataGridCellBackgroundBrushSelected/Focused/SelectedFocused/SelectedUnfocused`). Override tylko jednej zostawia brąz na drugiej. Spójny niebieski focus state wymaga override'u obu zestawów kluczy w obu theme dictionaries.

61. **Move Field "queue and compile" vs "execute immediately" — wybór sygnalizuje user'owi czy chodzi o pojedynczą zmianę czy batch.** Wcześniej Add/Drop było immediate ale Move było queue'd — niespójność która powodowała że user widział pole na miejscu, klikał ↑, nic się nie zmieniało wizualnie. Po decyzji "wszystko strukturalne idzie do bazy od razu, Rollback to escape hatch" wszystkie 3 mają identyczną semantykę. Jeśli kiedyś będzie potrzeba batch (drag-and-drop reorder z 5 polami) — `AddMovePending` zostało jako pure-API call, można zbudować nad nim batch-Compile workflow bez zmiany dotychczasowych ścieżek.

64. **DataGrid context menu + keybindings + double-click MUSZĄ wywoływać te same commands.** Inaczej zachowanie się rozjeżdża między input modes. Wzorzec: zdefiniuj N commands na VM, połącz każdą z trzech input ścieżek do tego samego command'a. ContextMenu MenuItem → `Command="{Binding XxxCommand}"`. DataGrid.KeyBindings KeyBinding → `Command="{Binding XxxCommand}"`. DoubleTapped handler w code-behind → `vm.XxxCommand.Execute(null)`. Wszystkie 3 sprawdzają `CanExecute`. Jeśli kiedyś trzeba dodać 4-tą ścieżkę (np. drag-and-drop) — wystarczy podpiąć do command.

71. **Avalonia ComboBox z `SelectedItem` TwoWay + `ItemsSource` którego bound-value NIE zawiera = cicha korupcja bound property.** Gdy `SelectedItem="{Binding X}"` a `X` nie jest w `ItemsSource`, ComboBox resetuje SelectedItem na null i TwoWay zapisuje null z powrotem do X. Dla pól pochodnych (np. `IsModified` porównujące X vs original) to fałszywe "zmienione". Dwa scenariusze: (a) bound value w innej reprezentacji niż items (pełny typ "VARCHAR(50)" vs bazowe items), (b) ItemsSource ładowane async PO bindowaniu (puste w momencie ataczowania → każdy lookup zwraca null). **Fix**: wrapper property z getterem zwracającym null-bezpiecznie + setterem ignorującym null/empty writeback (`if (value is null) return;`). Trzymaj prawdziwą wartość w osobnym property, nie pozwól ComboBoxowi jej nadpisać.

76. **A string `Content` on `CheckBox`/`Button` is access-key-processed — `_` becomes a mnemonic and is swallowed.** FluentTheme's `ContentPresenter` runs `RecognizesAccessKey` on string content, so `ID_NAGL` renders as `IDNAGL`. For data that may contain underscores (Firebird identifiers!), use an explicit `<TextBlock Text="{Binding Name}"/>` child instead of `Content="{Binding Name}"` — a control content isn't access-key-processed.

77. **`TextSearch.TextBinding` for a templated ComboBox must use `{ReflectionBinding}`, not element-syntax `<Binding>`.** With `x:DataType` on the host (compiled bindings), a `<TextSearch.TextBinding><Binding Path="Name"/></TextSearch.TextBinding>` compiles against the WINDOW's DataType (which has no `Name`) → `AVLN2000`. The TextBinding is evaluated per-ITEM, so it needs a runtime-resolved binding: `TextSearch.TextBinding="{ReflectionBinding Name}"`. Pair with `IsTextSearchEnabled="True"` to get type-ahead in a non-editable ComboBox.

80. **A star-width (`Width="*"`) DataGrid column SUPPRESSES the horizontal scrollbar.** The star column absorbs remaining width, so the DataGrid lays its columns out to fit the viewport and never reports content wider than the viewport — fixed/MinWidth columns past the edge get cut off with no way to scroll to them. For a wide grid that must scroll horizontally, give EVERY column a fixed/`MinWidth` (no star); the content width then exceeds the viewport and the built-in horizontal scrollbar engages. **Rule**: never use a star column on a DataGrid that needs horizontal scrolling.

95. **`DataGrid.ColumnReordered` + `DataGridColumn.DisplayIndex`/`Width`/`ActualWidth`/`Header` and `MenuItem.ToggleType`/`IsChecked` are all stable in Avalonia 12.0.0 — but `Visual.GetVisualRoot()` resolved awkwardly on `DataGrid` even with `using Avalonia.VisualTree;`; use `Control.IsLoaded` for an "already attached?" check instead.** Set `DisplayIndex` sequentially in increasing target order to reorder columns programmatically. Apply pixel widths as `new DataGridLength(px)` and reset to content sizing with `DataGridLength.Auto`.

97. **A thin (≈12px) clickable strip must NOT be a `Button` driven by `Click`.** Two failure modes: (1) `Button.Click` needs press AND release on the same control — a 1-2px pointer drift on a narrow target drops the release outside and cancels the click ("works on the Nth try"); (2) switching that `Button` to a bubbling `PointerPressed` handler fails entirely because `Button` marks `PointerPressed` handled in its own class handler before instance handlers run. Use a `Border` (doesn't swallow pointer input) with a `PointerPressed` handler — fires immediately on press, reliable every time. Reserve `Button`+`Click` for normal-sized targets.

99. **Resolve a right-clicked DataGrid cell via the public `DataGrid.CellPointerPressed` event, NOT grid-level `PointerPressed` + `DataGridCell.OwningColumn` reflection.** `DataGridCell.OwningColumn` is internal AND a grid-level pointer handler doesn't reliably resolve the cell on an EDITABLE DataGrid (the cell consumes/owns the gesture differently than on a read-only grid). The dedicated `CellPointerPressed` event is raised by the `DataGridCell` itself with public `Row` + `Column` on the args (`DataGridCellPointerPressedEventArgs`) — reliable, no reflection, fires before the ContextMenu opens.

111. **`DataGrid.IsReadOnly` does NOT disable controls living inside a `CellTemplate`.** It governs only the grid's own edit-mode machinery (double-click / F2 → swap to `CellEditingTemplate`), which covers plain text/date columns. Any *always-visible interactive control* injected into a `CellTemplate` (a `CheckBox` for booleans, a `…` Button for BLOBs — the gotcha #56 pattern) stays live regardless of `IsReadOnly`, so it must be gated on the capability itself (`CanEditData`) at template-build time — render a non-interactive read-only display (e.g. the `✓` glyph) when editing is disabled. **Rule:** when a column hosts a live control in its `CellTemplate`, gating the grid's `IsReadOnly` is not enough — branch the template on the edit capability.

124. **An always-visible interactive control (ComboBox/CheckBox) inside an `IsReadOnly` `DataGridTemplateColumn` bypasses the DataGrid edit cycle — `RowEditEnding`/`CellEditEnding` never fire for it.** Any "detect that the user changed this cell" logic hung off the grid's edit events will silently miss those columns. Route change-detection through the row VM's `PropertyChanged` (the bound property DOES update via TwoWay) instead of the grid's edit events. When that detection feeds a queue, make the enqueue idempotent-per-row (track + replace) so the VM-PropertyChanged path and any surviving `RowEditEnding` path can both fire without duplicating, and guard re-entrancy if the handler writes back to the row.

130. **To prompt asynchronously during window close, make `OnWindowClosing` `async void`, set `e.Cancel = true` BEFORE the first `await`, run the guard, then re-`Close()` behind a `_forceClose` flag.** Avalonia reads `e.Cancel` when the synchronous portion of the handler returns — an `async void` handler runs synchronously up to the first await, so setting `e.Cancel = true` there keeps the window open while the awaited guard/dialog runs. If the guard allows the close, set a `_forceClose` field and call `Close()` again; the re-entrant handler skips the guard and runs the persist path. Never try to await inside a synchronous closing handler without cancelling first — the window tears down underneath you.

134. **Lazy COUNT-only load is what makes the existing filter safe — the filter code was never the real problem, the eager-load was.** The metadata filter already (a) cost ∝ loaded leaves (unloaded groups hold one placeholder child) and (b) only auto-expanded LOADED groups. The "filter expands the whole tree and lags" symptom came entirely from eager-load making ALL categories loaded at connect. Once connect fetches only counts (groups stay unloaded with a placeholder), the same filter touches almost nothing and can't realize a storm. Lesson: before rewriting a hot path, check whether an upstream eager-materialization is what makes it hot — fixing the materialization can fix the symptom without touching the path. (Avalonia `TreeView` + nested `VirtualizingStackPanel` still estimates scroll extent poorly for huge expanded branches — that's the one piece lazy-load only mitigates, not cures; `TreeDataGrid` is the cure if ever needed.) **Superseded in part by the Etap 1 follow-up below** — gotcha #134's "lazy-load makes the filter fine as-is" was incomplete: with lazy-load the filter has *nothing loaded to search*, so it needed a session name cache to show per-category match counts. See #135.

138. **The Avalonia `TreeView` container does not accept keyboard focus — focus a `TreeViewItem` instead.** `tree.Focus()` is a no-op in practice (and headless-provably so: the focused element stays unchanged); the TreeView delegates keyboard focus to its item containers. To "return focus to the tree", focus a real `TreeViewItem` — the selected row's container (`FindTreeViewItemFor(tree, tree.SelectedItem)`) if there is a selection, else the first realized `TreeViewItem` descendant. Corollary for routed-event handlers attached to the TreeView: input only routes through the TreeView (so a tunnel handler on it fires) when a descendant **item** is focused — which is the normal case after a click, and the basis of the type-to-filter redirect.

154. **Filtering a virtualized `TreeView`/`ListBox` by toggling item `IsVisible=false` does NOT fix scroll performance — the hidden items stay in the `ItemsSource`, so the `VirtualizingStackPanel` still allocates/measures a layout slot for each one and computes the scroll extent across all of them.** On a 1500-row category filtered to a handful, ~1495 zero-height containers make scrolling jerky, the thumb wrong, and selection/hit-testing laggy. The fix is to filter the **collection** the panel binds to (keep an unfiltered master list; rebuild the displayed `Children` to matches only), so the VSP only ever sees rows that are actually shown. Corollary: any consumer that needs the FULL set (autocomplete, name resolution, bulk-"all") must read the master list, not the now-filtered displayed collection.

157. **Avalonia's `VirtualizingStackPanel` estimates scroll `Extent` from the AVERAGE height of *realized* rows (`≈ averageRealizedHeight × itemCount`) — so NON-uniform row heights make the extent (and the scrollbar thumb) swing wildly as you scroll, even with virtualization working correctly.** In the sidebar TreeView (nested VSP: outer + per-item) with an 8000-row expanded category, telemetry showed `extentH` lurching 521589 → 80690 → … → 25165 (`dExtent≈-6169` per realization) as the realized window changed from mixed node types (connections/folders/category-headers ~tall) to pure leaves (~short) — the thumb was ~0.1% of the track, re-sized every frame, and collapsed below the offset near the end → blank list. This is NOT a rebuild bug and NOT broken realization (the extent reflects the full count); it's the estimator reacting to a variable average. **Fix without abandoning the control: pin every row to ONE height** so the average is constant → `Extent = rowHeight × count` is exact and stable. Pin the TreeViewItem HEADER parts (`ContentPresenter#PART_HeaderPresenter` + `Grid#PART_Header`), NEVER the `TreeViewItem` itself (that clips the child subtree). Avalonia 12.0.0 has no "assume fixed item size" hint on VSP, so uniform actual heights are the only lever short of migrating to `TreeDataGrid` (single flattened VSP = exact extent by construction). Diagnostic fingerprint to confirm: `SCROLL … dExtent≠0` flagged `EXTENT RE-ESTIMATED DURING SCROLL` correlated with a changing `realized` count, plus `[Layout]Layout cycle detected … Track/VirtualizingStackPanel`. Corollary: an env-gated diagnostic that "captured nothing" may simply not have been enabled — default it ON during an active investigation (gotcha in ScrollTrace) so evidence isn't lost to a missed env var. **⚠️ CORRECTION (Round 4): the "pin every row height" fix was tried and DISPROVEN for EmberTern's sidebar — and reverted.** It addresses only a gentle average-height *drift*; the actual failure is far more severe: on a random-access thumb drag the nested VSP **derealizes the whole expanded subtree**, collapsing `Extent` to == viewport (`realized`≈4) and clamping the offset to 0, then rebuilds — a re-realization cycle uniform heights can't touch. When you see the extent hard-collapse to viewport (not a smooth walk), stop trying style/height fixes: it's the nested-VSP-in-TreeView random-access limitation, and the only real fix is a single flattened virtualized list (`TreeDataGrid`). Verify no EmberTern data recreation first (`Children`/`ItemsSource`/`IsExpanded` mutations during scroll — a `[rebuild]` line) to rule out a genuine app bug, then migrate.

159. **Avalonia 12.0.3 replaced the drag/drop data API — `DataObject`/`DragDrop.DoDragDrop`/`DragEventArgs.Data` are gone/obsolete; use `DataTransfer` + `DragDrop.DoDragDropAsync(PointerPressedEventArgs, IDataTransfer, …)` + `DragEventArgs.DataTransfer`.** For an intra-app drag that carries a live object (not OS-serialized), use `DataFormat.CreateInProcessFormat<T>(name)` + `DataTransferItem.Create(format, value)` / `dataTransfer.TryGetValue(format)` — the object travels by reference. `DoDragDropAsync` requires the originating `PointerPressedEventArgs`, so capture it at PointerPressed and start the drag from PointerMoved once past the movement threshold. Cross-control drag (sidebar → a single non-virtualized `TextEditor`) is the built-in API's happy path — distinct from the virtualized-`TreeView`-drop-target unreliability of gotcha #40; the sidebar's own reorder stays pointer-based.

160. **A `RadioButton` with a static `GroupName` inside a REUSED `UserControl` deadlocks the UI when >1 instance renders — a shared group across independent VMs fights the group manager in an infinite binding loop.** The first cut of `FilterPanelView` had two `RadioButton`s (`GroupName="filterCombine"`) for AND/OR with `IsChecked="{Binding !MatchAny}"` (one-way) + `IsChecked="{Binding MatchAny, Mode=TwoWay}"`. Avalonia scopes `GroupName` to the whole window, so when the same UserControl renders in several places (SQL Results + Table Data + View Data …) ALL the "AND" radios land in ONE group. The group manager permits only one checked member; it unchecks the others; each other's one-way `{Binding !MatchAny}` immediately re-asserts "checked"; the group unchecks again → an infinite check/uncheck loop that never lets `Dispatcher.UIThread.RunJobs()` settle → the app freezes the moment a detail view renders (and the headless probe hangs forever). A SINGLE instance is fine (self-consistent), which is why it slipped a solo build. **Fix:** don't put a grouped `RadioButton` in a shared UserControl — use a single `CheckBox` (unchecked = AND / checked = OR), which has no group and no cross-instance coupling. **Rule:** any control with window-scoped grouping (`RadioButton.GroupName`) is unsafe inside a control that can be instantiated more than once; prefer a `CheckBox`/`ToggleButton`/`ComboBox`, or generate a per-instance unique group name in code-behind. Diagnose a "renders once, hangs when reused / hangs in the headless probe that builds the real window" symptom by looking for shared window-scoped state in the reused view first.

164. **Avalonia 12 `GotFocus`/`LostFocus` args are `Avalonia.Input.FocusChangedEventArgs` (base `RoutedEventArgs`) — there is no `GotFocusEventArgs`.** A code-behind XAML handler typed `(object?, RoutedEventArgs)` binds to `GotFocus="..."` / `LostFocus="..."` fine (the base type is delegate-compatible with how Avalonia wires XAML events — same as the existing rename `LostFocus` handlers). Don't reach for a `GotFocusEventArgs` type (it doesn't exist → CS0246); use `RoutedEventArgs`. For "select all on focus", post the `SelectAll()` at `DispatcherPriority.Background` so it wins over the click's caret placement.

166. **To reuse the SAME shared VM/panel across multiple host views, set the child's `DataContext` from code-behind via `FindAncestorOfType<Window>()` — do NOT reach the window from XAML with `{ReflectionBinding DataContext.X, RelativeSource={RelativeSource AncestorType=Window}}` unless that pattern already exists in the codebase.** The one PerformancePanelView is hosted in three places (SQL Editor bottom panel + Procedure + Function detail sub-tabs), all bound to the single `MainWindowViewModel.Performance`. In the SQL Editor it's a direct `DataContext="{Binding Performance}"` (the host IS MainWindow). In the detail UserControls (whose own DataContext is the detail VM) the panel must reach the window's DataContext. The XAML `RelativeSource AncestorType=Window` + `ReflectionBinding DataContext.Performance` COMPILES and doesn't crash, but if it silently fails to resolve you get an empty panel — and a headless/8-second smoke can't catch it (it needs a live connection + Execute + tab-switch). The codebase has NO precedent for that XAML shape, but it DOES have a proven one: the detail code-behinds already do `this.FindAncestorOfType<Window>()?.DataContext is MainWindowViewModel mainVm` in `OnAttachedToVisualTree` to wire editor services. Reuse it: give the hosted panel an `x:Name`, `FindControl` it in the ctor, and assign `panel.DataContext = mainVm.Performance` in `OnAttachedToVisualTree`. **Rule:** prefer the established code-behind ancestor-walk for cross-DataContext wiring in a reused control; only use a `RelativeSource`/`$parent` XAML binding when the same shape is already proven elsewhere in the app.

174. **AvaloniaEdit 12.0.0 ma pełny wbudowany Find+Replace (`AvaloniaEdit.Search.SearchPanel`) — NIE pisz własnego silnika.** `SearchPanel.Install(editor)` podpina panel i auto-wire'uje Ctrl+F; `IsReplaceMode` przełącza tryb Replace (Replace/Replace All), `MatchCase`/`WholeWords`/`UseRegex` + „x z y” gotowe. **Ctrl+H nie jest auto-wire'owane** — dodaj własny KeyDown handler na edytorze (`IsReplaceMode=true; Open()`). Kontekstowe Ctrl+F rób w window-**tunnel** handlerze: gdy fokus jest w `TextEditor` (`FindAncestorOfType<TextEditor>(includeSelf:true)`), **nie obsługuj** — zdarzenie tuneluje do SearchPanel edytora; inaczej zrób swoje (np. fokus filtra). Gate akcji edycyjnych (Replace/Comment/Format via `Document.Replace`) na **runtime `editor.IsReadOnly`** (data-bound, przełącza się), nie na statycznej fladze — programowy `Document.Replace` omija read-only. Weryfikuj API vendora przez reflection (MetadataLoadContext), nie z pamięci.

180. **`ElementName` (and any name-scope binding) does NOT resolve inside a `ContextMenu`/`Flyout` — they live in a separate popup namescope, not the window's.** A `ContextMenu` inherits the DataContext of the control it's attached to, so `{Binding SomeCommand}` against that DataContext works; but `{Binding DataContext.X, ElementName=RootWindow}` silently binds to null (the menu item just does nothing — no error). To reach a command that lives on a DIFFERENT VM than the menu's DataContext, expose a forwarding property on the DataContext VM (`public IRelayCommand FooCommand => _owner.FooCommand;`) and bind by inheritance — never reach across with `ElementName` from inside a popup. This bit the trigger "Selected" bulk items (bound to the explorer VM via `ElementName`) — they no-op'd until re-routed through a node-VM forwarding property.

183. **The global `Style Selector="TextBlock"` (`Foreground=ForegroundBrush`) overrides a Button's inherited `Foreground` on its label text — a `Button.primary` with a `TextBlock` child needs its OWN descendant style to color the label.** `Button.primary` sets `Foreground=OnAccentBrush`, which the ContentPresenter should pass down — but a Style setter beats inheritance, so every `TextBlock` gets the dark `ForegroundBrush` instead (the Execute label was too dark on the blue button in Light theme; the play `SvgIcon` + the "F5" hint looked right only because they set their foreground explicitly). Fix in the SHARED style, not a local override: `Style Selector="Button.primary TextBlock" { Foreground = OnAccentBrush }` (defined after the global `TextBlock` style so it wins; a child TextBlock with its own local `Foreground`, like the subtle hint, still wins over it). Same trap applies to any button-class whose content is text on a non-default fill.

199. **AvaloniaEdit's `CompletionWindow.CloseOnFocusLost` is a `protected virtual` get-only property — to
stop the list dismissing when the user drags its OWN scrollbar, subclass and override it to `false`.** The
scrollbar-thumb drag opens the list as a separate popup window that deactivates the parent window;
AvaloniaEdit's `ParentWindow_Deactivated` → `CloseIfFocusLost` then closes the list mid-scroll (the reported
bug). There is no public setter, so the intended hook is a tiny `CompletionWindow` subclass overriding the
getter. This disables ONLY the focus-lost close path — `CloseAutomatically` (caret leaving `[Start,End]`),
Escape, item selection, and non-matching input still close it. Accepted trade-off: clicking a non-editor
control while the list is open leaves it open until the caret next moves. Reflect the real API surface
(get/set visibility) before assuming a property is settable — the member merely appearing in a dump doesn't
mean it has an accessible setter.

200. **AvaloniaEdit's `CompletionWindow` does NOT apply an initial filter on `Show()` — it filters only on a
subsequent `CaretPositionChanged`.** A window opened with a prefix already typed before the caret
(`StartOffset < caret`) shows the entire list until the caret next moves. Always call
`window.CompletionList.SelectItem(document.GetText(StartOffset, caret - StartOffset))` right after `Show()`
to apply the initial filter (`SelectItem` is public, `IsFiltering` defaults true, and it templates itself if
the listbox isn't realized yet). Verified against the 12.0.0 source; do not assume the control self-filters.

201. **`TextView.VisualLines` THROWS `VisualLinesInvalidException` when `!VisualLinesValid` (a re-measure is
pending).** Never touch `VisualLines` (even `.Count`) without first checking `VisualLinesValid`. A
double-click (selection change) or a just-activated/not-yet-laid-out editor leaves the lines invalid, so any
caret-rect/popup-placement helper must guard `VisualLinesValid`, optionally `EnsureVisualLines()` (public,
but throws `InvalidOperationException` if called mid-Measure — catch it and fall back), and only then read
`VisualLines`. `GetVisualPosition` does not throw on invalid lines but returns garbage, so guard there too.

203. **AvaloniaEdit's `CompletionList` control template is JUST the inner `CompletionListBox` — the
`CompletionList` never paints its own `Background`, so styling `aecc|CompletionList` Background is a
VISUAL NO-OP.** (Verified against the 12.0.0 `CompletionList.xaml`: the `ControlTemplate` is a lone
`<cc:CompletionListBox Name="PART_ListBox">` with no `Border`/`Panel` binding `{TemplateBinding
Background}`.) The visible surface is the `CompletionListBox`, a Fluent `ListBox` (`BasedOn` the ListBox
theme), which paints the Fluent *default* ListBox background — near-white in the Light theme, so the popup
blends into the `#F3F3F3` editor. This is exactly why the "give the completion popup an elevated
background" fix "didn't work". Fix: style **`aecc|CompletionListBox`** (Background `ElevatedPanelBrush` +
`BorderBrush`/thickness), not `CompletionList`. An application-level `Style` setter outranks the Fluent
ListBox `ControlTheme`'s default, so it wins. **Rule:** before styling a third-party templated control,
confirm which part actually renders the surface — a `Background` on a control whose template never binds it
paints nothing.

204. **The double-click INSERT/VALUES column helper (P6) silently does nothing on a fresh editor because
the signature engine needs the target table's COLUMNS, which aren't cached yet.**
`SignatureHelpEngine.GetSignature` builds an INSERT signature from `meta.GetColumns(table)`; with no cached
columns it returns `null` (not an empty-param signature), so `NavigationController.IsInsertHelperSignature`
is false and the popup never appears — while the app no longer crashes (gotcha #201), it shows nothing.
Columns are lazy per-table and NOT warmed by the double-click path (unlike dot-completion and typed
signature help, which already do the warm-then-rebuild dance). Fix: added
`SignatureHelpEngine.TryGetInsertTargetTable` (returns the target table even when columns are absent) +
`SqlCompletionController.WarmColumnsAndRebuildAsync`, and the double-click handler now warms the columns,
rebuilds the shared model, and retries the signature before giving up. **Rule:** any editor feature that
consumes lazily-loaded metadata must warm-then-retry on a cache miss, not treat "not cached yet" as "not
applicable".

205. **A metadata-changed subscription routed through an attach-time hook that internally guards on a
possibly-null field can LATCH "subscribed" while actually subscribing nothing — permanently dropping the
signal.** `SqlCompletionController.SubscribeMetadataChanged` set `_metadataSubscribed = true` and called the
injected `subscribeMetadataChanged` lambda; the main window's lambda was `h => { if (_currentVm is not null)
_currentVm.Metadata.ObjectsChanged += h; }`. If `_currentVm` was still null when the editor attached, the
`+=` was skipped but the latch flag was set anyway, so the `ObjectsChanged → rebuild-model` handler was
never attached and never retried. Because categories prefetch sequentially (Tables first, Views/Procedures
LAST), the model was built with only the early categories and never rebuilt when the late ones loaded →
"tables resolve, views/selectable-procs in FROM don't (no colour/Ctrl-nav/Quick Info)" even though the
binder resolves them perfectly given the metadata (pinned by `EditorModel_RebuildsAgainstGrownMetadata` +
`SemanticHighlightConsistencyTests`). Fix: wire the metadata event to the STABLE VM in
`OnDataContextChanged` (where the VM is guaranteed non-null and outlives the window), via the new public
`SqlCompletionController.NotifyMetadataChanged()` — not through the attach-time hook. **Rule:** subscribe a
long-lived signal to a lifecycle where the target is guaranteed present; never let a "did I subscribe?" flag
be set independently of whether the subscription actually happened (same family as #187/#179 — the value is
right but nothing tells the consumer to re-read it).


206. **The double-click INSERT/VALUES helper must derive its offset from the POINTER position, not
`_editor.CaretOffset` — on a real double-tap the caret is not reliably at the clicked value when
`DoubleTapped` fires.** The P6 helper read `_editor.CaretOffset` in `OnDoubleTapped`, assuming "the
double-tap has already placed the caret in the word". In practice the caret can still be at the previous
single-click position when the gesture bubbles, so `SignatureHelpEngine.GetSignature` ran at a stale
offset (often not an INSERT position) → no signature → no popup — the helper "never appears" even though
the whole decision→popup path is correct (proven headlessly by driving `TryHandleDoubleClick(offset)` with
a known-good offset: the popup opens for both cached and warm-then-retry cases). Fix: `int offset =
OffsetAt(e.GetPosition(_editor)) ?? _editor.CaretOffset;` (the same pointer→offset mapping Ctrl+hover uses).
The `DoubleTapped` event itself DOES fire (the earlier crash proved the handler ran), so `handledEventsToo`
is unnecessary. **Rule:** for a pointer-driven editor feature, resolve the document offset from the
pointer, never assume the caret has already moved to match the gesture.

207. **"Ctrl+Click opens the object" does NOT prove the semantic model resolved it — `NavigationController`
falls back to a NAME-based open (`_openByName` → loaded metadata) when the model can't resolve.** So a
report of "Ctrl+Click works but the name isn't coloured / no Ctrl-hover / no Quick Info" for a view or
selectable procedure in FROM is the fingerprint of a model that did NOT resolve the object (only the
name-fallback fired) — NOT a highlighter bug. Proven for this project by three headless probes:
`EditorModel_RebuildsAgainstGrownMetadata` (binder resolves view+proc given metadata),
`SemanticHighlighter_BareFromView_PaintsObjectColour_NotLocal` (a resolved bare-name `FROM view` is painted
`IconColor_View`, beating the overlapping implicit-table-reference "local" brush), and the AvaloniaEdit
`TextView.Redraw()` behaviour (`ClearVisualLines()`+`InvalidateMeasure()` → the colorizing transformer
re-runs, so the `ModelUpdated`→repaint IS effective). Conclusion: binder, classifier, brush-mapping, and
repaint are all correct; a live "view/proc coloured like a plain identifier" is a **metadata-not-reaching-
the-model** timing issue (see #205), never the highlighter. **Rule:** when a feature has a silent
name-based fallback, don't treat "the fallback-capable action works" as evidence the primary (model) path
ran — instrument the primary path (or a probe) before blaming a downstream consumer.


208. **Statements separated only by newlines (no `;`) collapse into ONE statement — so only the first is
semantically analysed (coloured / Ctrl-nav / Quick Info); the rest read as plain text.** The parser's
`ScanPlain` ends a plain statement at the next top-level `;`; with none, it runs to EOF, and `Classify`
types the whole document by its FIRST keyword (e.g. a script beginning `EXECUTE PROCEDURE …` becomes one
`ExecuteProcedureStatement`), so the binder only resolves that first statement's objects. Proven by
runtime instrumentation (`[HL] parserStatements=1` for a 4-statement no-`;` document) and a unit test.
The `;`-only segmentation is the executor boundary authority (shared via `SqlStatementSplitter`, gotcha
#192) and MUST stay byte-identical — do NOT change `SqlParser.Parse` for this. Fix: a **lenient** parse
used by the READ-ONLY semantic model only (`SqlParser.Parse(text, lenient:true)` → `SemanticModel.Build`)
that also ends a plain statement at a top-level statement-start keyword
(`SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/MERGE/EXECUTE/CREATE/ALTER/DROP/RECREATE/WITH`), paren+BEGIN/CASE/END-depth
aware, with continuation guards so it never false-splits `WITH cte AS (…) SELECT`, `INSERT … SELECT`,
`… UNION SELECT`, `CREATE VIEW … AS SELECT`, or `MERGE … WHEN … THEN INSERT/UPDATE`. Safe because a
mis-split in a read-only model only weakens IntelliSense, never corrupts code (§0) — unlike the executor,
where a mis-split would corrupt execution. **Rule:** don't assume one editor buffer = one statement; and
when a boundary heuristic is too risky for execution, apply it only to the read-only analysis layer.


209. **A bare `new Popup { PlacementTarget = editor } + ((ISetLogicalParent)popup).SetParent(editor) +
IsOpen = true` can be INVISIBLE on the real desktop even though `IsOpen=True`, the child is laid out
(non-zero Bounds), `IsVisible=True` and `Opacity=1`.** Runtime instrumentation proved it: the INSERT/VALUES
helper popup opened with `childBounds=0,0,205.6,358.4`, `childVisible=True`, `childOpacity=1`, and stayed
open ~1–2 s — yet nothing appeared on screen. The separate `PopupRoot` (its own top-level) is the failure
surface: on desktop it can render off-screen / behind / clipped despite every "is it open/visible" signal
being true, and headless tests can't catch it (no real window compositor). Fix: host anchored editor
content in the editor's **`OverlayLayer`** (`OverlayLayer.GetOverlayLayer(editor)`, a `Canvas` — position
the card with `Canvas.SetLeft/SetTop` in overlay coordinates via `editor.TranslatePoint(caretPoint,
overlay)`, and clamp it inside `overlay.Bounds`). The overlay renders IN the window, on top, no PopupRoot —
the same reliable in-window layer AvaloniaEdit's completion uses. **Rule:** for editor-anchored popovers,
prefer the OverlayLayer; treat a bare `Popup` whose only tree link is `SetParent` as unreliable, and verify
visibility on the real desktop (log `IsOpen`/`Bounds`/`IsVisible`), not just in a headless test. The other
custom popups (hover tooltip, Quick Info, Peek, rename) still use the bare-Popup pattern — migrate them if
they show the same symptom.


210. **A signature-help-style popover's lifetime must be CONTEXT-driven (re-query the engine on caret
move), NOT tied to a raw `CaretOffset` — AvaloniaEdit nudges the caret 1–2 chars after a double-click's
word selection.** The INSERT/VALUES helper opened correctly but vanished ~70 ms later: the double-click
selects a word and AvaloniaEdit settles the caret to the token boundary (`armedAt=375 → caretMoved=377`),
and an offset-equality dismiss (`caret != armedOffset → close`) killed the helper even though the user was
still in the same VALUES argument. Runtime logging proved it: `reason=caret-moved` on every single
dismissal, never a real context change. Fix: on each caret move, call
`SignatureHelpEngine.GetSignature(model, caret)` again and keep the helper open while it is still an INSERT
signature for the SAME target table (`Label`) — rebuilding the card to follow the active argument — and
close only when the context genuinely changes (leaves the INSERT value/column region → engine returns
null/other kind, another statement/table, Escape, editor detach). This is how VS/Rider/IBExpert drive
parameter info and is immune to editor caret jitter. Pinned by
`ConnectionExpandBindingProbe.InsertHelper_DoubleClick_OpensPopup_CachedAndWarmed` (caret jitter + argument
change keep it open; leaving the context closes it). **Rule:** anchor an editor popover to a *semantic
context* re-evaluated from the model, never to a captured caret offset.


211. **The per-trigger-character metadata "warm-then-retry" hacks (#204's double-click warm, the
Parameter Helper's `WarmForSignatureAndRebuildAsync`) were symptoms of one missing piece — a generic
"warm what the model references" pipeline — not independent bugs each needing their own fix.** Package 5
added that missing piece: `EditorLanguageService.BeginWarmReferencedMetadata()` fires after EVERY model
build (debounced parse, `EnsureFreshModel`, and `RefreshModelWithMetadata` alike), collects every object
the just-built model references (`CollectReferencedObjectNames` — FROM/JOIN/DML targets, trigger NEW/OLD
targets, every resolved schema object), warms columns/routine-params/rich-detail for whichever aren't yet
cached (`MainWindowViewModel.WarmReferencedAsync`), and — if anything was newly loaded — rebuilds once
more against the now-complete snapshot, looping while a re-warm is pending (a category loading mid-warm)
so it converges. `ModelFresh` now gates on a metadata GENERATION as well as the text version, so a
deliberate Ctrl+Space rebuild after a background category load is never suppressed. Net effect: (a) the
#205 "latched subscription" class of bug is gone at the root (the model is proactively kept complete, not
patched per-symptom); (b) the character-specific warmers — dot-completion's column warm, the double-click
INSERT helper's `WarmColumnsAndRebuildAsync` (#204), and the Parameter Helper's own warm — **still exist**,
but are now legitimate SYNCHRONOUS top-ups for the one exact site the user is looking at right now (they
call `EnsureFreshModel()`/`RefreshModelWithMetadata()` synchronously because a keystroke-driven popover
can't wait for the async background pass), not independent guess-and-retry hacks; they fill the SAME
caches the general pipeline fills, so a cache hit in one is a cache hit in all. **There is one metadata
cache + one generic warm pipeline; the synchronous per-feature warms are a deliberate latency
optimization on top of it, not a parallel workaround.** Rule: when two "different" bugs turn out to have
the same "metadata wasn't there yet" fingerprint, look for the missing generic mechanism before patching
each call site again.


212. **A "never stall" loop guard (`if (i == before) i++;`) that advances an index without ever emitting
the skipped token is a silent §0 (Paramount Law) violation, not a harmless safety net.** Found while
scoping P8; **fixed in P8 Krok 0 (Formatter Safety), 2026-07-13.** `SqlFormatter.EmitPsqlUnit` returns
without consuming `i` when it sees a stray `END` (`if (IsWordTok(sig[i], "END")) return;`) — correct ONLY
when the caller is an open-`BEGIN` loop that will itself consume that `END` as the block close.
`FormatPsqlBody`'s own top-level loop and `EmitPsqlUnit`'s BEGIN-block loop both call `EmitPsqlUnit` and,
seeing no progress, fell back to `if (i == before) i++;` to avoid an infinite loop — which discarded the
token instead of emitting it verbatim. Net effect: malformed/incomplete PSQL with an extra/unmatched `END`
(a very ordinary mid-edit state) had that `END` silently vanish from the formatted output — real user-typed
text disappearing, the exact class of bug §0 exists to prevent. **Rule: an anti-stall guard that skips a
token must ALSO emit it (verbatim, unformatted) — "don't loop forever" and "don't drop the token" are two
separate obligations, and a fix for one must not silently reintroduce the other.** The fix has two layers:
(a) each stall guard now calls `EmitStrayToken` (emit the unplaced token verbatim on its own line, then
advance) so the emitter is lossless by construction — the same "don't understand it → reproduce it 1:1"
contract `RawStatement` gives at the statement level, applied at the token level; (b) a **checked
invariant** wraps the whole formatter: after formatting each statement its output is compared
lexeme-for-lexeme to its input tokens and, on any mismatch, the statement is kept verbatim, and a
script-level backstop returns the input unchanged if the final result still differs by even one lexeme
(covers the string-level long-line wrapping stage). §0 is now a guarantee, not a hope — the formatter
either reproduces every lexeme or leaves the fragment/document unchanged, even for input it cannot model.
Pinned by `SqlFormatterSafetyTests` (adversarial malformed corpus: lexeme-preservation + no-throw +
idempotency). **Rule: a formatter (or any code-rewriter) that re-emits from a model instead of copying the
source must VERIFY its output preserves the input, and fall back to verbatim when it doesn't — don't trust
every emit path to be individually perfect.**

216. **Trigger context variables (NEW/OLD/INSERTING/UPDATING/DELETING) are highlighted via the SEMANTIC model, not the lexical keyword catalog — because none of them are reserved words in Firebird.** A tempting "just add them to `FirebirdSyntax`" (lexical) fix would colour a legitimately-named column/table `INSERTING` or `NEW` everywhere, and would colour them even in a procedure body where they mean nothing. Instead the binder (`SemanticBinder.BindTriggerDefinition`) declares INSERTING/UPDATING/DELETING as `TriggerPredicateSymbol` into the trigger's routine-body scope (alongside the existing NEW/OLD `RecordAliasSymbol`), so a bare occurrence resolves ONLY inside a CREATE TRIGGER; the classifier maps both symbol kinds to `SemanticHighlightClass.ContextVariable` (coloured like CURRENT_DATE etc. — the Function/context-constant palette). Rule: when a "keyword-like" word is context-dependent and not reserved, model it in the binder (scoped resolution) rather than lexing it as a global keyword — the semantic layer is where "only here does this word mean that" belongs. (This is the general form of the P5a lesson: highlighting quality comes from the model resolving the reference, not from a bigger keyword list.)

218. **A body-only editor (the Easy-mode routine/trigger editors) establishes NO surrounding-object scope — context that lives in the header/grids must be seeded as AMBIENT symbols, or the semantic model can't see it.** The trigger detail editor edits only `begin…end` (no `CREATE TRIGGER … FOR <table>` header), so the text parses to an `AnonymousBlockStatement` → `BindAnonymousBlock`, which never declares NEW/OLD or INSERTING/UPDATING/DELETING and has no target table — so trigger context variables were never classified/highlighted there even though they worked in the full-text SQL Editor (`BindTriggerDefinition`). The symptom read like "the highlight colour isn't applied", but the real cause was "the model never resolved the symbol" (same tell as gotcha #205/#207: no colour + no hover + no Quick Info, yet a name-based fallback still works). Fix uses the existing `SemanticBinder.Bind(script, metadata, ambientSymbols)` seam (built originally so Ctrl+Space could see a routine's params/DECLAREs that live in the grids, not the body text): `TriggerDetailTabViewModel.BuildAmbientSymbols` seeds `RecordAliasSymbol("NEW"/"OLD"){TargetTable}` + `TriggerPredicateSymbol(...)` into the model's root scope, visible to EVERY client (highlight/completion/quick-info/nav) with no second code path. **Rule: whenever a body-only editor needs to resolve names that are defined by its surrounding object (params, NEW/OLD, trigger predicates, package context), seed them via `BuildAmbientSymbols` — do NOT synthesize a fake header or add a feature-specific shim.** Before "the colour is wrong", verify the reference resolves at all (a headless `SemanticModel.Build(text, meta, ambient)` test is the fastest check).

217. **Uppercasing an identifier that EmberTern GENERATES is only §0-safe for a *regular* (unquoted-able ASCII) identifier — a case-sensitive/quoted one must be preserved verbatim.** Easy-mode wants generated DDL in the house UPPERCASE style (gotcha #141 already coerces typed names upper). `DdlGenerator.PresentIdentifier` folds a regular identifier to UPPER + bare — safe because Firebird folds an unquoted regular identifier to upper anyway, so only the presentation changes, never the resolved object — but leaves a leading-digit / space / non-ASCII (i.e. genuinely case-sensitive, must-quote) name verbatim and QuoteLight-quoted, because uppercasing `"myName"`→`"MYNAME"` would reference a DIFFERENT object. Keep this presentation rule (generation-only) strictly separate from `SqlFormatter` (which formats existing SOURCE and preserves its own casing — a future per-token casing option lives there, not here); the two are different responsibilities and must not reach into each other. Residual boundary (documented, accepted): an externally-created quoted-lower-case *regular* domain is presented upper — consistent with the app-wide uppercase convention, and the pre-existing code already emitted it wrongly (bare lower-case → Firebird folds → not found).


219. **"Wired in the one shared seam ⇒ every SQL surface has it" is FALSE — the main SQL Editor does not go through `SqlEditorBehavior.Attach`.** That installer is shared by the **object** editors (Procedure/Function/Trigger/View/Package detail + Script Executor); `MainWindow` hand-wires the main SQL Editor itself (its own `SqlCompletionController` + `SemanticHighlighter` / `NavigationController` / `OccurrenceHighlighter` / `EditorSearch`), because its callbacks must be null-safe against a `_currentVm` that attaches later, not a stable `vm`. Stage 7 / S3 attached `SquiggleRenderer` in `SqlEditorBehavior.Attach` only — with a comment (and commit message) asserting "every SQL surface" — so **the most-used editor in the app rendered no squiggles at all** and nobody noticed, because the manual QA pass happened in an Easy-mode procedure editor. The diagnostics were computed there the whole time (`EditorLanguageService` runs on the controller, which that editor does have); only the paint was missing — which is exactly why it was invisible in tests and in code review. **Rule: adding an editor capability means attaching it in BOTH places, and the check is `grep` for the call sites, not the comment above them.** The duplicated wiring is the root cause and deserves its own consolidation milestone; until then, treat `SqlEditorBehavior.Attach` as "the object editors' installer", never as "the seam". Same failure mode as #205/#207: the feature was correct and complete; only its *installation* was missing on one surface. **✅ RESOLVED (2026-07-17, debugger milestone D3):** the two copies are consolidated. `MainWindow` no longer hand-wires the intrinsic editor block in its ctor; it calls the SAME `SqlEditorBehavior.Attach(_editor, _currentVm)` the object editors use, once its VM arrives (first non-null `OnDataContextChanged` — the window's `DataContext` is set after construction, so the shared path, which needs a stable non-null VM, waits for it: "subscribe once the VM arrives"). The old bespoke metadata handlers (`OnMainEditorMetadataChanged` / `OnMainEditorMetadataReady` / `WarmReferencedMetadataAsync`) and the private `CreateMetadataSnapshot` / `EnsureColumnsAsync` / `EnsureRoutineParametersAsync` forwarders are deleted — the controller's own metadata hooks (bound to `vm.Metadata` inside `Attach`) took over their whole responsibility. **The intrinsic block only** was consolidated (completion / highlighting / navigation / squiggles / related-elements / language-completion / typing-ergonomics / search); the genuinely per-host wiring — `DiagnosticsPanelHost.Track` (F8 + diagnostics panel, with per-host reveal), `AmbientModelRefresh` (routine editors), `SqlSnippetDropTarget` (context varies) — stays a caller responsibility, since it truly differs per host and was never the duplication risk. The old "add it in BOTH places" rule is therefore retired: there is now **one** `Attach` seam for the intrinsic block. See `docs/history/19-firebird-debugger.md` (D3).

220. **A rule guarded by `IsEffectivelyVisible` silently inverts its meaning when its consumer lives in a SIBLING TAB.** The object editors' `ActiveEditor` is `_focusedEditor is not null && _focusedEditor.IsEffectivelyVisible ? _focusedEditor : <mode primary>` — correct for Alt+F Format (never format an editor you can't see). Reusing it to drive the **Diagnostics panel hosted as a peer tab** would have been a silent bug: selecting the Diagnostics tab *hides the editor tab*, so the guard can never hold while the panel is on screen, and the rule collapses to the mode-primary branch **always** — the Cursors/Subprograms editors could never appear in the panel, and "focus an editor → the panel retargets" would be unobservable by construction. It would have looked like it worked (the body/source case, i.e. the common one, is indistinguishable). **Rule: before reusing a visibility-guarded rule, check whether the NEW consumer can be on screen at the same time as the thing being guarded.** The fix was a deliberate, documented sibling rule (`LastFocusedSqlDocument` — sticky last-focused SQL editor, no visibility guard, cleared on mode flip / object rebind), NOT a tweak to `ActiveEditor`, because the two encode genuinely different intents. Bonus: dropping the guard also removed a dependency on how Avalonia's TabControl realizes hidden tab content. See `docs/design/editor-stage7-diagnostics.md` §8.2.1.

221. **`DispatcherPriority.Background` is BELOW `Input` — so any state a repeated keypress reads as its anchor must be written SYNCHRONOUSLY, never inside the posted continuation.** The established "jump to an offset in another tab" idiom (`PackageDetailTabView.OnNavigateToMember`) posts the whole caret+scroll+focus block at `DispatcherPriority.Background`, and for its trigger (a one-shot double-click on a tree node) that is exactly right: the tab switch must lay out before the target editor can scroll. Copying it wholesale into Stage 7 / S5's `F8 = next diagnostic` would have introduced a **hold-F8-does-nothing** bug: F8 picks its target by comparing the diagnostics to `editor.CaretOffset`, and Avalonia's priority order (`… > Render > Loaded > Input > Background > ContextIdle > …`) means a *queued second F8* is dispatched **before** the first jump's Background continuation runs — so the second keypress reads the pre-jump caret and re-selects the SAME diagnostic. The split that works: set the caret + selection **synchronously** (it needs no layout — it is a document operation, valid even on an unrealized editor), and post **only** `BringCaretToView()` + `Focus()`, which genuinely do need a laid-out `TextView`. **Rule: when you defer UI work, ask what the NEXT event will read. Anything that is an input handler's anchor is state, not presentation, and belongs on the synchronous path — deferring it makes rapid repeats silently idempotent.** The tell is a feature that works when clicked slowly and stalls when a key is held.

222. **A diagnostic and its explanation live on an UNRESOLVED symbol — so any "explain this squiggle" surface gated on symbol resolution shows nothing exactly where it is needed most.** `QuickInfoEngine.GetQuickInfo` is *symbol*-shaped: `ReferenceAt(offset)` → `Symbol` → card, returning **null** unless the identifier **resolved**. The Ctrl+hover tooltip inherited that gate (it keyed off `NavigationEngine.TargetAt` returning a *navigable target*), which is fine for "check this column's type" — and useless for diagnostics, because **`ET0001`/`ET0002`/`ET0003`/`ET0005`/`ET0007` all fire on references that by definition did NOT resolve**. Hovering an unknown table therefore produced *nothing*: no target, no symbol, no tooltip, precisely at the error. The fix is to change the **gate**, not the renderer: *"is there a resolved symbol **or** a diagnostic at this offset?"* — and to treat "both sections present" as the *rare* path, not the design centre (`ET0006` is the main way to reach it, since it spans a VALUES list that can contain resolved symbols). **General rule: before reusing a symbol-driven lookup for an error-driven feature, check whether the errors are ON symbols at all.** Corollary for the interaction design: Ctrl+hover *means* "this leads somewhere" — an unknown object leads nowhere, so putting the explanation behind Ctrl would attach a navigation affordance to the one thing that cannot be navigated. Information belongs on plain hover; Ctrl stays actionability (frozen §9.4, confirmed not amended). See `docs/design/editor-stage7-diagnostics.md` §15.1.1 / §15.3.

223. **A caret-driven `IBackgroundRenderer` must repaint with `TextView.Redraw()`, not `InvalidateVisual()` — and a "skip if spans unchanged" guard must skip ONLY empty→empty.** Stage 8 / M1's `RelatedElementsRenderer` (bracket / BEGIN-END matching) recomputed on caret move and repainted via `TextView.InvalidateVisual()`. Symptom (manual QA): bracket matching did **not** activate on the FIRST call the caret landed on right after connect; clicking a *different* call worked, and returning to the first then worked too. Root cause: right after connect the window is still settling, and a plain `InvalidateVisual()` can run its render pass before the `TextView` has (re)built its visual lines, so `Draw` hits `VisualLines.Count == 0` and paints nothing; once layout settles a later repaint paints, and thereafter all do. The matcher was never at fault — it is a pure function of (text, caret, model) and returns the same pair every time (proven by a headless caret-driven test), so a first-vs-later difference can only be in the paint. **Two fixes, both needed:** (a) repaint with `_editor.TextArea.TextView.Redraw()` — it invalidates measure, forcing a visual-line rebuild + render, so a background renderer always draws against valid lines (this is exactly why `SemanticHighlighter` uses `Redraw()` for its non-content repaints, while `SquiggleRenderer`'s `InvalidateVisual()` is fine because it is triggered by `ModelUpdated`, which rides an already-settled content redraw); (b) a change-detection guard that skips repaint on unchanged spans must skip **only** the empty→empty case (plain-text caret movement) — skipping an unchanged *non-empty* set turns a single missed paint into a permanent one, because the caret sitting still on the same pair recomputes the identical spans and the guard suppresses the retry until the caret visits another pair and comes back (the exact "works after I click elsewhere and return" tell). **Rule: for a caret/selection-driven overlay renderer, repaint with `Redraw()`, and never let a diffing guard skip a non-empty state.**

224. **To make Tab do something other than indent in an AvaloniaEdit editor, intercept it in a TUNNEL `KeyDownEvent` handler and set `Handled` — a bubbling `+=` handler is too late.** Language Completion (Stage 8 / Language Expansion) expands an armed construct on Tab (`if`→`if () then`). AvaloniaEdit's built-in Tab-indent runs from the `TextArea`'s own key handling (a class handler / key binding), which in the bubbling phase executes **before** any instance handler added with `editor.TextArea.KeyDown += …` — so a bubbling handler sees the tab already inserted. The completion controller gets away with bubbling `+=` for Ctrl+Space/Escape because those are not built-in editing keys; Tab is. Fix: `editor.AddHandler(InputElement.KeyDownEvent, OnPreviewKeyDown, RoutingStrategies.Tunnel)` — Avalonia's `KeyDownEvent` is registered `Tunnel | Bubble`, so the tunnel (preview) handler runs top-down before `TextArea` processes the key; setting `e.Handled = true` there preempts the indent. **Only consume Tab when a construct is actually armed** (re-derive it from text+caret in the handler) — otherwise return without handling so normal indent still works — **and bail when the completion list is open** so the list keeps Tab for accept. Same tunnel pattern already used by `SearchableComboBox` / `GlobalSearchTabView` / the window key handler. Rule: overriding a built-in editor key (Tab, Enter, Backspace) needs a tunnel handler; a bubbling subscription silently loses the race.

225. **AvaloniaEdit's `TextEditor` is NOT focusable — `editor.Focus()` is a no-op returning `false`, and keyboard focus lives on the `TextArea`.** Measured, not inferred: in a headless probe `editor.Focusable` is **`False`**, `editor.Focus()` returns `False`, and the focused element stays `null`; `editor.TextArea.Focus()` returns `True` and the focus manager then reports `AvaloniaEdit.Editing.TextArea`. Consequences: (a) a focus guard on an editor feature must read `editor.TextArea.IsKeyboardFocusWithin` (or `TextArea.IsFocused`) — `editor.IsFocused` is **always false**, so a guard written against it disables the feature permanently while compiling and testing green; (b) any test that needs to type into an editor must call `editor.TextArea.Focus()`, never `editor.Focus()`, or every subsequent key/text input goes nowhere. `editor.IsKeyboardFocusWithin` *is* true when the TextArea has focus (it is an ancestor), so it works too, but the TextArea is the precise seam. This is exactly the #199 class of bug — the member exists and compiles, but its runtime contract is not what the name suggests. Found by Language Completion's hint focus-guard: the headless probe failed on the FIRST assertion, which is the only reason the guard did not ship dead.

226. **One `HeadlessUnitTestSession` per test PROCESS is a hard requirement, not tidiness — AvaloniaEdit builds its caret/editing `KeyBinding`s as STATIC lists, owned by the thread of whichever session first constructs a `TextEditor`.** Each `HeadlessUnitTestSession.StartNew(...)` starts its own UI thread; `ConnectionExpandBindingProbe` was calling it **24 times** (once per test) and mostly got away with it, because most tests only touch per-instance objects. The moment a test sends a real `KeyDown` into a `TextEditor` from a *later* session, `TextAreaInputHandler.TextAreaOnKeyDown` → `KeyBinding.TryHandle` → `KeyBinding.get_Gesture()` → `AvaloniaObject.GetValue` throws **`InvalidOperationException: The calling thread cannot access this object because a different thread owns it`**. It is not an input-injection artefact: raising the event directly on the `TextArea` on the current thread fails identically, because the *KeyBinding objects themselves* belong to another thread — so no injection style avoids it. Fix: one `private static readonly HeadlessUnitTestSession SharedSession = HeadlessUnitTestSession.StartNew(typeof(HeadlessAppEntry));` reused by every test in the class (which is what #94 always meant). Bonus: the partition got ~3× faster (16s → 5s) with 24 fewer UI threads. **Rule: never call `StartNew` per test; share one session for the whole process.**

227. **A completion window's "don't show an empty list" guard must test the FILTERED view (`CompletionList.CurrentList`), not the candidate set (`CompletionList.CompletionData`) — the filter runs AFTER the guard.** `SqlCompletionController.FinishWindow` checked `CompletionData.Count == 0`, then called `window.Show()`, then `ApplyInitialFilter` → `CompletionList.SelectItem(prefix)`, which narrows the list to the already-typed prefix (itself a necessary step — see the comment there: AvaloniaEdit only filters on a subsequent `CaretPositionChanged`, so a window opened with text before the caret would otherwise show everything). So the emptiness check ran on the full candidate set and the narrowing that empties it happened afterwards: a prefix matching **nothing** produced a visible, empty, bordered rectangle over the code that still owned Tab/Enter. Latent for a long time and only exposed when `BEGIN` left the keyword vocabulary (it had always matched `begi` before). Two fixes: after the initial filter, close if `CurrentList.Count == 0` — but **only when a filter actually ran**, since with no prefix `CurrentList` is not the list's state yet and checking it would close every legitimate full list (Ctrl+Space); and on typing into an open window, re-check (the caret moves before `TextEntered`, so AvaloniaEdit's filter has already run by then). Read `CurrentList` rather than re-deriving the match rule — `GetMatchQuality` is private, and any re-implementation would silently disagree with what the list actually draws. **Rule: when a control filters after you populate it, "is it empty?" must be asked of the control's own post-filter view.** **⚠ SUPERSEDED (2026-07-17, #232):** both mechanisms this entry describes — `ApplyInitialFilter` and `CloseIfNarrowedToNothing` — are **deleted**. The controller now pre-filters through `CompletionMatcher` and switches AvaloniaEdit's filter OFF, so nothing filters after we populate: there is no unfiltered moment to patch up, and "the filter emptied it" cannot happen because an empty match result means the window never opens. The *rule* still stands wherever a control does own its own filtering; it just no longer applies here. Note the shape of the lesson, though — this was a workaround for a foreign filter we had not yet taken ownership of, and taking ownership deleted it rather than adding to it.

228. **Splitting responsibilities by VOCABULARY does not split them by KEYSTROKE — the other tool can still own the same keys through data you did not filter.** Language Completion owns SQL constructs, so `CompletionEngine` stopped offering their trigger words (`select`, `where`, …) as keywords. That looked like separation and wasn't: typing `select` still popped the identifier list, because a **procedure named `SELECT_PRACOWNIKOW`** matched the prefix — a *name*, which no keyword filter would ever remove. And since an open completion list owns Tab, Tab inserted the procedure instead of expanding the construct, with the construct's hint never even shown (it suppresses itself while the list is open). The frozen design had said this all along — "*where a construct may begin, the identifier list doesn't auto-pop*" — and only the vocabulary half had been implemented. The real gate is **grammatical position**: the auto-pop path asks the same pure `LanguageConstructResolver.Resolve(text, caret)` the hint asks and returns if anything is armed (explicit Ctrl+Space still opens the list — the developer may always overrule the grammar). Two corollaries: (a) both tools must consult **one** resolver, or they will disagree about what is armed; (b) once a tool owns a word exclusively, an **under-arming** grammar gate becomes a dead zone rather than a harmless miss — the other tool is no longer there to cover it, so the gate must be verified across every position the construct may legally begin in, and should widen rather than narrow when uncertain. **Rule: before declaring one tool the owner of a keystroke, enumerate every source that can still respond to it — vocabulary, names, and the trigger paths themselves.**

229. **A line comment's trivia span ends BEFORE the newline, so a caret at its `End` is still inside the comment — a block comment's `End` is not.** `SqlTrivia` for `-- note ` covers exactly `-- note ` (the doc says so: "up to, but not including, the end of line"), so the strict `caret > Start && caret < End` test that is correct for a `/* … */` reports *false* for a caret sitting at the end of a line comment — which is precisely where a developer types. Typing Ergonomics' delimiter pairing hit this immediately: `-- note |` + `(` inserted `()` **inside a comment**, because the caret was on the boundary rather than strictly within it. The asymmetry is real and intentional, not a lexer bug: a line comment runs *to* end-of-line (everything typed at its end stays commented), while a block comment's `End` is past the `*/` and is code again. **Rule: when consuming trivia spans, treat `LineComment` as inclusive at `End` and `BlockComment` as exclusive.** Same shape as the unterminated-literal case beside it (a string the developer hasn't closed runs to the end of the text, so the caret at its `End` is still inside it) — in both, "the region extends past where its span stops". *(`DelimiterPairing.IsInsideLiteralOrComment`)*

231. **When a batch's pass/fail drives control flow, compute it from the execution loop's OWN tally — never from the batch dialog's `SuccessCount`/`FailedCount`, which are updated via `IProgress` and can lag.** Save-and-close reuses `RunBatchWithReportAsync` with a custom `executeAsync` delegate that drives each dirty editor's `SaveAsync`; the orchestrator's `progress.Report(...)` marshals through `Progress<T>` (captured `SynchronizationContext`), so `vm.AddResult` — and therefore the counters — run on a posted callback that, with no `SynchronizationContext` (headless tests), lands on the thread pool AFTER the loop returns. `SaveDirtyEditorsAsync` originally decided "may I close?" from `report.SuccessCount == count` and read stale zeros. Fix: tally `succeeded`/`firstFailedTab` inside the loop itself (plain locals) and decide from those; the VM counters are for the DIALOG only. Corollary worth knowing: `RunBatchWithReportAsync` calls `vm.RequestCancel()` immediately after `await show(vm)` returns, so **without a `BatchResultsRequested` handler the token is cancelled the instant the modal would have closed** — only *synchronously-completing* steps finish (real usage is safe because the modal `ShowDialog` blocks until the batch is done; a headless test with async steps would see them cancelled). **Rule: a batch's own progress UI is a view, not a source of truth for the caller's decision — keep the decision in the loop.** *(`MainWindowViewModel.SaveDirtyEditorsAsync`)*

232. **AvaloniaEdit's `CompletionList` filters by SUBSTRING by default, and `IsFiltering = false` is the only way to take the list back — after which the inner ListBox renders your `CompletionData` verbatim, in your order.** Its match scale (the *private* `GetMatchQuality`, no override hook) runs exact → StartsWith → CamelCase → **Contains**, and anything scoring ≥ 1 is displayed. On a metadata-heavy database that silently turns interactive completion into a catalog search: typing `cont` lists `XXX_PS_CONTRACTORMAP`, `GEN_XXX_PS_CONTRACTORMAP`, `MON$CONTEXT_VARIABLES` and every `FK_…CONTRACTOR…` index beside the `CONTAINING`/`CONTINUE` the user actually meant. **Measured**, not assumed (headless probe, 12.0.0): with `IsFiltering = false`, `SelectItem()` degrades to a selection move (`SelectItemWithStart`), `ListBox.ItemsSource` stays bound to `CompletionData`, and **AvaloniaEdit's quality re-sort stops too** — so the kind ranking you supplied survives (a table ranked 3.0 renders above a keyword ranked 1.0, where the quality sort would have floated the exactly-matching keywords first). Consequences once it is off: the list is yours to narrow on every prefix change (drive it from `Caret.PositionChanged` — it covers backspace/delete/paste/click, where `TextEntered` covers only typing), and `ICompletionData.Priority` goes **inert**, since AvaloniaEdit read it only from the filter path. **Rule: interactive completion is a PREDICTION engine (prefix), not a search engine (substring) — substring lookup over the catalog is Global Search, a different workflow and a different window.** *(`SqlCompletionController.OpenWindow`; supersedes the workarounds in #200 and #227)*

233. **A correct, unit-tested Core component that nothing calls is indistinguishable from a regression — and the green suite is what hides it.** The prefix-first `CompletionMatcher` shipped with 8 passing tests pinning exactly the behaviour the user wanted, and the user still saw substring matches for weeks: the wiring step had been deferred to a later session, so the component sat unused while AvaloniaEdit's filter did the real work. Every signal said healthy — build 0/0, suite green, the rule demonstrably implemented and reviewable in Core — because the tests asserted the *component*, never the *surface*. The user reported it as a regression, which it wasn't; nothing had ever worked. Two habits fall out: (a) when a milestone deliberately lands a foundation and defers the wiring, the parked state belongs somewhere that gets read on a bug report — CLAUDE.md's "Current state" said so, and checking it turned a hunt into a 5-minute diagnosis; (b) the pin for "Core decides X and the UI shows exactly X" must be taken **at the surface** — here a headless probe reading the *rendered* ListBox, deliberately with no fallback to the `CompletionData` we supplied, since reading your own input back asserts nothing. **Rule: a unit test proves a rule exists; only a test at the surface proves the rule is in force. If a component's whole value is being called, assert that it is called.** *(`ConnectionExpandBindingProbe.Completion_PrefixFirst_ListsOnlyStartsWithMatches`)*

234. **AvaloniaEdit's `CompletionList.CompletionData` is a plain `List<ICompletionData>`, NOT an `ObservableCollection` — mutating it after the window is shown updates NOTHING on screen.** `OnApplyTemplate` binds `ListBox.ItemsSource = _completionData`; a `List<T>` raises no `CollectionChanged`, so the rendered rows freeze at whatever was bound when the template applied, while the collection quietly reads something else. AvaloniaEdit never mutates it: `SelectItemFiltering` builds a **fresh** `List` and assigns `ListBox.ItemsSource = listBoxItems` — **that assignment IS its refresh mechanism**. So switching `IsFiltering` off (#232) silently removes the only thing that ever re-assigns `ItemsSource`, and a `Clear()`+`Add()` in its place changes the data and nothing else: typing `n.id_nagl` kept the row `ID_AKWIZYTOR` on screen while `CompletionData`, `SelectedItem` and even `ListBox.ItemsSource` all correctly read `ID_NAGL`. **The tell was `ListBox.ItemCount` frozen at its opening value** — the control had never heard of the change. Fix: after populating `CompletionData`, hand the ListBox a snapshot (`listBox.ItemsSource = new List<ICompletionData>(data)`), mirroring AvaloniaEdit's own pattern; keep `CompletionData` content-identical to it, because with `IsFiltering=false` AvaloniaEdit's `SelectItemWithStart` indexes into `CompletionData` and applies the index to the ListBox. **Rule: before mutating a control's bound collection, confirm it is observable — if the type can't broadcast, assignment is the only refresh.** *(`SqlCompletionController.Populate`; the flip side of #232)*

235. **Reading a control's `ItemsSource` proves what it was TOLD, not what it SHOWS — assert the realized containers.** The probe for #232/#233 read `ListBox.ItemsSource` and passed while the screen was visibly wrong (#234), because `ItemsSource` was the very `List` we had just mutated: the assertion was reading our own input back through the control's front door. It was written explicitly to avoid that trap ("no fallback to the `CompletionData` we supplied") and fell into it one level down — the object identity, not the property name, is what made it circular. The hook that catches it walks `ContainerFromIndex(i)` after `UpdateLayout()` and reads each realized container's item; against the stale-list bug it reported `[ID_AKWIZYTOR]` where `ItemsSource` said `[ID_NAGL]`. **Rule: for a "the UI shows exactly X" claim, the assertion must traverse the visual tree; any hook whose value can be satisfied by the data you passed in is not evidence.** Corollary: virtualization means containers are only realized for the visible window, so assert them on small fixtures and keep the ItemsSource check alongside for content. *(`SqlCompletionController.RenderedRowsForTest`; a sharper instance of #233)*

297. **A code-behind handler that resolves its subject from `sender`/`e.Source`'s `DataContext` does NOTHING when the control sits on a parent's DataContext — and the control still looks and behaves correct.** Settings Center's numeric fields commit on blur and Enter through `if (sender is Control { DataContext: NumericSettingViewModel row }) row.Commit();`. The fields were bound `Text="{Binding PreviewRowLimit.EditText}"` against the *page's* view model, so their own `DataContext` was the page — the pattern never matched, and every field accepted typing, displayed the number, echoed it back, and **persisted nothing**. Nothing throws, nothing warns, the binding is genuinely correct, and a view-model test proving `Commit()` works passes: the only broken link is between the control and the object the handler expected to find on it. Fix: give the group the row as its own `DataContext` plus the matching `x:DataType` (`DataContext="{Binding PreviewRowLimit}" x:DataType="vm:NumericSettingViewModel"`, the shipped `DataImportTabView` idiom — six occurrences there), after which the inner bindings shorten to `{Binding EditText}` and the handler matches. **Rule: when a handler identifies its subject through a control's `DataContext`, that is a real contract between the XAML and the code-behind — state it where the control is declared, and pin it with a test that drives the CONTROL (raise the event / send the key) rather than one that calls the view model's method.** The general tell for this family: the UI does the visible half of the job and silently skips the persisting half. *(`SettingsWindow.axaml` + `.axaml.cs`; the same "wired?" shape as #233 and etap 4's shared-`GroupName` trap)*

298. **A single-line Avalonia `TextBox` does NOT claim `Enter` — so do not reach for a tunnelled handler (#224) on the assumption that it does; measure first.** Mid-etap the diagnosis was *"`TextBox` handles Enter in its own class handler, which runs before an instance handler on the same element, so a XAML `KeyDown="…"` never fires"*, and a window-level `RoutingStrategies.Tunnel` handler was added on that basis, then scoped back down by inspecting `e.Source` so it would not become a window-wide Enter grab. **Probed on the headless session (Avalonia 12.0.0): with `AcceptsReturn=false` the bubbling instance handler on the box runs with `Handled=false`, and the key reaches the Window still unhandled.** `TextBox.OnKeyDown` consumes `Enter` only when `AcceptsReturn` is true. The field's real fault was #297, a different bug with the same symptom — which is exactly how a false premise survives: it was adopted *because* the plain handler appeared not to work.
